# Backlog — the one ordered list of work (31 Oct; Phase 1 of `design/simplify-plan-2026-10-31.md`)

One queue for everything: **Now** (in order, being done), **Next** (in order, starts when a Now row in its area is done), **Later** (not scheduled). Owner of the order: the tech lead. Each row names its area and "Done when". Bugs keep their detail in `qa/BUGS.md`; designs and plans in `screens/<item>/`. Every open item of `WAVE-1.md`, `SCREENS.md`, `REQUESTS.md`, `LEAD.md` and the lane files is carried here; the item-by-item check is `design/backlog-carry-over-2026-10-31.md`.

**Done when, for every row:** acceptance checks pass with a pass and a fail test per business rule, CI green on the PR, the reviewer approves, merged; then the demo (:4400) runs it and qa tests it on `main`. US$0 unless the founder approves a stated cost.

## Owners (current owner per open row; moved from the team board, P-224; the tech lead keeps it current)
One owner per row and per shared file; never build another owner's rows. Under the lean model (`WAY-OF-WORKING.md`) each builder holds one row at a time; the owners below are as of 1 Nov night and shrink as rows merge. Shared-file rules are in `WAY-OF-WORKING.md`.

| PF-23 | runtime (running it: logs, metrics, alarms) | **Logs nobody can read personal data from, metrics and alarms (map row 13).** (1) **One logger:** NestJS `ConsoleLogger` `json: true` behind `LogPort`; every line has time, level, `correlationId` (from `X-Request-Id` or new), `firmId` when known, route, status and duration; a redaction step at the logger drops personal fields by name and pattern (email, phone, names, document numbers, tokens, `Authorization`, cookies, message bodies, subjects, attachment names; SA-11-37, BR-16): entries carry record ids and rule names only. (2) **Metrics** as CloudWatch embedded-metric-format lines (request count, errors and latency per route; job runs, failures and age per queue; mail sent and bounced; audit-write failures). (3) **`AlertPort.raise(kind, detail)`** (fake in CI, CloudWatch alarm via the log line in the cloud) for: audit-write failure after retry and a hash-chain break (SA-11 BR-2, BR-5), a stuck job (SA8-D40's constant), and a privacy-request or breach clock near its date (NFR5-14). (4) `GET /api/health/live` and `/api/health/ready` (database and job queue). (5) **CI check "no personal data in logs"** (Doc-1 release gate): run the suites with a capturing logger and fail on any seeded personal value in the captured lines. **Touch:** new `api/src/platform/observability/*.ts`, `api/src/features/platform-observability.ts`, `tests/check_pf23_observability.ts`, one CI step; the one-line logger hook in `api/src/main.ts` (release holder's OK as a shared file) | tested with a pass and a fail each: a request logs one JSON line with its correlation id, and a supplied `X-Request-Id` is kept; a seeded email, phone, name and bearer token in a request, an error and a job never appear in captured output; the no-personal-data check fails on a deliberately leaking line (shown once, then removed); an audit-write failure raises one alert and a second within 5 min raises none (Sample); ready answers 503 when the database is down; metric lines parse as embedded metric format | NFR5-14; Doc-1 §8.4; SA-8-27; SA-11-37, BR-2, BR-5, BR-16; Founder log L304-306 · **waits on:** PF-4 (actor and firm on the request), PF-14 (job metrics) · decisions: **error reports in CloudWatch, not Sentry** (Sentry stores data in the US or the EU, never Canada; map row 1 keeps data in Canada) · reviewer only · **size:** 2 PRs (logger and redaction with the CI check; metrics, alerts and health). The cloud half (log groups in ca-central-1, 90-day retention, alarms, the on-call address) is PF-23c, with PF-8. · Source: SA-11-37, SA-8-27, NFR5-14, SA8-D40 |

In the cloud the deploy injects them from AWS Secrets Manager (SA-8-1, "supplied at deploy"; PF-8's task definition); rotation is an engineer runbook with no downtime (SA-8-4, SA8-D1), written as a new runbook RUNBOOK-secrets.md in the deploy folder. (2) **`SecretBoxPort`** for a firm's or a person's own connection credentials (FO-3v's Zoom tokens, FO-3s's calendar tokens later): AES-256-GCM by `node:crypto`, key from the loader, **key version stored with each value** so a rotation re-encrypts by a `JobPort` job; shown masked, never decrypted into a response. | **FO-7mv** | schema + runtime (client money: the "To move" list) | **(Renamed from Lead B (5)'s FO-7m by Lead 53, 15 Nov: FO-7m is already the monthly trust check.)** **The "To move" list from trust to operating, safe against returns and corrections (AG-6-73; B-18, B-19 from #1758).**<br>(1) `money_move_batch` and `money_move_line` in FO-7a's money area file. Its own migration folder, with a stamp later than `main`'s newest (P-600), additive.<br>(2) **Cap:** a `TRUST_APPLIED` move line is at most its withdrawal less the money returned from it. A return of money already moved is refused unless that move is undone first. `money_move_line_write` takes the party trust lock.<br>(3) **Correction kinds:** a deposit correction carries `BOUNCED`, `WRONG_ACCOUNT` or `ENTRY_ERROR`. Only a `WRONG_ACCOUNT` correction, linked to its deposit, permits a `WRONG_ACCOUNT` move out of trust, capped at that correction's amount. `BOUNCED` and `ENTRY_ERROR` never permit a move.<br>(4) The party's trust balance never goes below zero (P-607), **except a reversal, which FO-7a2 accepts below zero and lists in `money_trust_shortfall` (P-634 (1)); FO-7a2 merges first.**<br>**Touch:** the money area file and one migration folder, `api/src/client-money/` move files (as FO-7a names them), `tests/check_fo7mv_move_list.ts` | `DW1`: "withdraw 100, move 100, return 100" three times on one 100.00 invoice moves 100.00 once; the second move and the return of moved money are refused (B-18). `DW2`: the cap and the trust lock hold under two parallel moves. `DW3`: a bounced cheque corrected −300 (kind `BOUNCED`), then a 300 move → refused (B-19 a). `DW4`: a 200 deposit refunded and then corrected −200, then a move → refused (B-19 b). `DW5`: a `WRONG_ACCOUNT` correction of 150 permits a move of 150, and 151 is refused. `DW6`: no path other than a reversal (P-634) takes a party's trust below zero. `DW7`: the migration test follows rev 16.7. | **Source:** AG-6-73, P-603, P-607; #1758 round 4 (B-18, B-19) · **waits on:** FO-7a merged · decisions: correction kinds (Lead B, row owner, 15 Nov; reversible, best practice: a correction states why) · **breaker: yes (money); builder, reviewer and breaker at high effort; the release holder reads the migration (P-423)** · **size:** 1 PR |

* **Rev 14 (Lead B (3), P-584 module homes, P-585, P-586):** **What changed and why.** P-584 (Lead 41, from A51 rev 3 §12) makes **messages and templates (email, WhatsApp) with the consent gate, automation, custom fields and views, client portal login and the complaints register platform services** (Lead B's under P-558), so the case side never depends on the front office to reach a client. P-585 gives Lead B map row 62 (client portal) and row 43 (calendar sync). P-584 (10): `money_*` tables join PF-12's walls now. P-584 (9): the listed one-line hooks (contracts §5, PF-3's case-feed line) are the written exception to one change, one module; a new hook needs both owners. Binding text: contracts §9.
* **Rev 14 (Lead B (3), P-584 module homes, P-585, P-586):** **Duplicate check (14 Nov ~10:0x IST):** `main` has no sending layer, consent gate, template store or automation engine: only the demo inbox stand-in (`api/src/person-link.ts` `DemoInboxController`, `demo_message` in `links.prisma`, SI-20), which the platform's `local` adapter keeps working. BACKLOG: map rows 45-49 had no row (case side, unshaped); FO-11 and FO-12a named "map rows 48/49" as the case side's. Open PRs: none on these areas.
* **Rev 14 (Lead B (3), P-584 module homes, P-585, P-586):** **Size:** no new scope (rows 45-49, 36, 62 were already in the map's 881); about 9-11 PRs move into Lead B's lanes (Hypothesis).
* **Amended rows (rev 14):**
  * **FO-1a:** drops `fo_consent` and `fo_consent_wording` (now `msg_consent` and `msg_consent_wording` in PF-19a); `fo_web_form` keeps a reference to the wording version. Brief updated.
  * **FO-2:** writes the consent given on the public form through `ConsentPort.record` in its one transaction; **waits on PF-19a** as well as FO-1a and PF-4. Brief updated.
  * **PF-L:** also adds `nodemailer` and `@aws-sdk/client-sesv2` (map row 48's pick) with STANDARDS §1 entries in the gate file. Brief updated.
  * **PF-4b:** `MailPort` is created there as the interface for staff and system email with the `local` adapter (demo inbox); PF-19b later routes it through `MessagePort`. No brief change needed beyond that note (added).
  * **PF-12:** **`money_*` tables join the walls now (P-584 (10))**, with `msg_*`, `auto_*`, `plat_complaint*` and every later PF/FO table; **no money route before money walls:** FO-7b waits on PF-12 (with FO-7a merged), so PF-12 moves up to run right after FO-7a. A table added after PF-12 enables its policy in its own migration (same pattern, PF-12's test extended).
  * **FO-11:** registers its WhatsApp channel in PF-19b (platform), not in a case-side file; its "map rows 48/49" waits become PF-19b; the release-side line OK is no longer needed. Touch list: `api/src/front-office/whatsapp/*.ts` plus one registration line in `api/src/platform/messages/channels/` (Lead B's own file).
  * **FO-12a → PF-20; FO-12b, FO-10, FO-12c** stay in the front office and register with PF-20 (FO-12c sends through PF-19b); **FO-12s** keeps Add to follow-up, Enrolled in, message many and the assignment panel; its settings part moves to PF-20s.
  * **FO-13:** drops the complaint tables (now PF-21); keeps `fo_conflict_check` and `fo_identity_check`. **FO-13k → PF-21. FO-13s** keeps the Conflict and Identity panels; the complaints list moves to PF-21s. **FO-13i:** the identity check's result (method, date, by; never the document) is carried on `prospect.won` (P-584 (8)); contracts §2.6.
  * **FO-7r (P-586 (5) item 14):** adds **AG-6-30** (every list, ledger and statement paginated and filtered on the server, no in-memory scan; 10,000 invoices → page 1 within the read budget; a request without a page size gets the default) and **AG-6-29's facts** (refunds, credit notes, write-offs and trust movements kept as separate facts; a disputed invoice stays in ageing; dashboards are AG-14's, not here).
  * **PF-18a (P-586 (5) item 15):** reconciled with PF-3: **PF-3's firm-wide business-hours and closed-days keys are the firm's single office's values** (no copy, no second store); PF-18a's per-office hours and closed days apply only once a second office exists; adds `platform_staff.office_id` (a host's office; none → the firm's zone) and `OfficeClockPort.closedDaysFor(staff, date)`, which FO-3's slot service calls (one call in FO-3's files, Lead B OK as owner of both) so **the host's office decides closed days (AG-4-60)**; business hours never limit slots (AG04-D22).
  * **FO-5p:** stays for the retainer and consultation agreement `.docx`; the email and WhatsApp template preview is PF-19c's.
* **Shaped next (owners named by P-584/P-585; rows two ahead):** PF-4p client portal identity (a client pool walled from staff, P-584 (4); map row 62's login part; the portal's pages stay with the module that owns each) · PF-22 custom fields and views with per-module registration (P-584 (2); cards to read first) · FO-3s calendar sync, two-way, behind a `CalendarPort` (map row 43, P-585 (4); also brings Meet and Teams links to FO-3v) · PF-19m connected mailbox with messages (map row 52, P-584 (3); Google's review is an outside clock) · FO-15 AS-1 record and screen (P-584 (7): the rules run in the configurator through its door; the front office never imports the engine).

### Rev 16.5 (14 Nov 20:27 IST, Lead B (3); copied by Lead 49 from eca729c7e): the environment setting fails closed (Lead 49, from PF-4 #1744)

**Why:**
* PF-4's `identityConfig` defaults `PLATFORM_ENVIRONMENT` to `local`, and `local` allows the local sign-in stand-in.
* A production deploy that forgets the setting would run as `local`.

**Best practice:** fail closed. Never default to the most permissive setting.
* **PF-24w (new, S, reviewer only):**
  * after PF-4 and PF-24 merge, PF-24's configuration loader takes over PF-4's environment reads (`PLATFORM_ENVIRONMENT`, `PLATFORM_IDENTITY`, `PLATFORM_LOCAL_SECRET`, the Cognito settings);
  * `PLATFORM_ENVIRONMENT` becomes **required, with no default**: missing → the app refuses to start, naming the setting;
  * `start.sh`, `deploy/compose.test.yml`, CI and the test helpers set `local` or `test` explicitly.
  * **Touch:** `api/src/platform/identity/port.ts` (the read only), `api/src/platform/config/*.ts`, those launch files (one line each; the release holder's OK as shared files), `tests/check_pf24w_environment.ts`.
  * **Done when** (a pass and a fail each): an unset `PLATFORM_ENVIRONMENT` stops start with its name; `local` with `PLATFORM_IDENTITY=local` starts; `production` with `PLATFORM_IDENTITY=local` is refused (already PF-4's rule, kept); every existing suite and the demo reset pass with the explicit settings.
  * **Waits on:** PF-4, PF-24.
* **PF-8 (cloud deploy, only named so far): Done when adds:**
  * the task definition sets `PLATFORM_ENVIRONMENT=production` and `PLATFORM_IDENTITY=cognito` explicitly;
  * the deploy smoke test proves the running service refuses the local sign-in stand-in (a local token → 401) and that `/api/health/ready` reports `environment: production`, never `local`.

### Rev 16.8 (15 Nov 07:4x IST, Lead B (4); copied by Lead 51 from e71fa7dff): follow-up rows from the review Minors (FO-5f, FO-3f, PF-4f)

**Why:** the Minors left open on FO-5 part 2 (#1777; lists in comments 6883913744, 6884091038, 6884260179), FO-3 (#1760, the PR body's "Open items for Lead B") and PF-4t (#1775 round 2, m1 and m2) become rows, so nothing found in review is lost. One row per area, each S, reviewer only unless marked. None needs a migration.

**Lead B decisions inside these rows (P-566 (3), best practice with evidence):**
* **An edit that ends a sent request writes an event.** Today it writes only an audit line, so nothing outside FO-5 learns that the signing request is void. New name `RETAINER_REQUEST_ENDED: "retainer.request_ended"` in `ports/events.ts`, payload `RetainerEventPayload` with `version` = the ended version and a `reason` of `UPDATED`. FO-6's brief already puts the `SigningPort` call inside FO-5's transactions; it adds the cancel in the same step for this edit and for Withdraw.
* **The stale check covers the state, not only the version (B-25).** Send does not raise the version, so an edit made on a Draft page passes after a colleague sent it, and ends the request. Edit and Withdraw also require `expectedStatus` (the status the page showed); a mismatch is 409 `CHANGED`. No migration: the pair (version, status) already names one state.
* **Signed offline on paper signed against an earlier version.** The paper is the legal record, so the record must file exactly what was signed (the B-24 rule). Signed offline takes an optional `signedVersion` (default: the current one). When it names an earlier version, the service writes a new version that copies that version's values, fees and file (marked "copied from version k, signed on paper"), and files that as signed, in one transaction. History stays forward-only (part 1's triggers unchanged), and the newer unsigned versions stay visible, marked "not signed". The panel shows the chosen version's fees and says plainly that the newer version will not be the signed one. `expectedVersion` stays required as the stale check.
* **HTML inside a PDF wrapper (B-15) is an accepted risk, no fix:** downloads already send `Content-Disposition: attachment` and `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`. FO-5f adds the test that asserts both headers on the signed-paper download, so the protection cannot be removed silently.
* **FO-3's slot read limit is per IP only** (R2-1: a per-firm read cap let a few addresses close a firm's page). Agreed on #1760; Lead B notes it in contracts §0.6.

| Row | What | Touch | Done when (a pass and a fail test each) | Waits on |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **FO-5f** | runtime (retainer hardening, FO-5 part 2's Minors) | 1. `RETAINER_REQUEST_ENDED` written by an edit that ends a live request (above). 2. `expectedStatus` on Edit and Withdraw; Withdraw also requires `expectedVersion` (B-25, the Withdraw gap). 3. `signedVersion` on Signed offline (above). 4. Placeholders in equations (`m:t`) and drawings or SmartArt (`a:t`) are found and **refused** with a plain message ("Placeholders inside equations, drawings or SmartArt are not filled. Move it into normal text."), never printed blank (r3-m1 / B-21). 5. The placeholder cap is counted **before** docxtemplater compiles, from the text runs, and a refusal says why (B-19 rest). 6. Parts that lie about their size are a 422 "This file is damaged or is not a Word file", never a 500 (B-20). 7. Name lengths are checked after NFC normalization, so a name that grows past 120 is a 422 (B-22). 8. The glossary part is not read for placeholders, since it is never rendered (B-23). 9. An agreement's "missing" list uses the placeholders of the template version it was made from, not the template's newest (Devin 4575579359). 10. The `tx()` log line carries only the error code and the constraint name, never Prisma's `meta` (r3-m2). 11. A refused download says "No such file" (B-26). 12. The shared upload filter's message in `main.ts` names no size ("This file is too large for this upload."); each route's own limit message names its number (B-8; one line in `main.ts`, flagged in the PR body for the release holder's read as a shared file). 13. The B-15 header test above. | `api/src/front-office/retainers/*.ts`, `ports/events.ts` (one name), `api/src/main.ts` (one line), `tests/check_fo5_retainers.ts` | each numbered item has its pass and fail test in `check_fo5_retainers.ts`; two staff on the same retainer: Draft page edit after a colleague's Send → 409 `CHANGED`; Signed offline naming version 1 while version 2 exists files version 3 = copy of 1, marked signed, and the audit names 1 and 3; a request ended by an edit writes exactly one `retainer.request_ended`; `graph.mjs --check` and the type check pass. **Breaker: yes** on items 2 and 3 only (money and the signed record). | FO-5 part 2 (#1777) on `main` · Source: AG-5-65, AG-5-50, AG-5-47, AG-5-49, AG-5-9, AG-5-3, AG-5-34, AG-5-16, AG-5-28, AG-5-39, AG-5-64; P-566 (3) (the Lead B decisions in rev 16.8); PR #1777 review and break reports |
| **FO-5f2** | runtime (retainers: template safety) | **A firm's Word template can never take down the API, and no unknown placeholder prints blank (FO-5f item 5's rest; review M-1 and N-1, breaker round 3).**<br>(1) **Allowlist before any parse:** a template is accepted only when every part in `[Content_Types].xml` is one of the types a Word template uses: main document, header, footer, footnotes, endnotes, styles, settings, font table, theme, numbering, web settings, core, app and custom properties under `docProps/`, relationships, and images. Every templated part must be named in the standard place. An Override typing a `.rels`, `[Content_Types].xml`, `customXml/` or any unlisted part as a templated type → 422 "This Word file has parts a template cannot have". `[Content_Types].xml` and each `.rels` are refused over 256 KB **before** they are parsed (N-1).<br>(2) **Isolation:** every docxtemplater `compile`, render and placeholder read runs in one `worker_threads` worker with `resourceLimits` (Sample 256 MB old space) and a 5 s timeout. A breach kills the worker only, the request answers 422 "This Word file is too complex to use as a template", and the worker restarts. The API process never runs `compile`.<br>(3) **Placeholders from the library:** inside the worker, the template's tags come from docxtemplater's own parse (`InspectModule` / `getAllTags`), so every tag it will fill is checked against the placeholder list, including property-text tags, CDATA and customXml. An unknown tag → 422 naming it.<br>(4) **No delimiter change:** the options set `delimiters` to `{ }` and turn off the change-delimiter syntax. A `{=` in the text → 422.<br>(5) The brace pre-count from FO-5f stays as a cheap first gate.<br>**Touch:** `api/src/front-office/retainers/docx.ts`, a new `api/src/front-office/retainers/docx-worker.ts`, `tests/check_fo5f2_template_safety.ts` | `DW1`: every way in from the hand-over (`.rels`-named header, `[Content_Types].xml`-named part, customXml typed core/diagramData, the six round-3 ways) → 422, with the API still answering a concurrent page read. `DW2`: a 9 KB `[Content_Types].xml` with 2 M `<a/>` and a 25 MB one → refused before parse, in under 1 s (N-1, Minor). `DW3`: a template built to exhaust memory inside an allowed part kills only the worker (422), and the next request works. `DW4`: `{client.passport}` in `dc:title` in a header, `Company` in settings, CDATA in a header, and customXml → 422 naming it. `DW5`: `{=<< >>=}` → 422 (each delimiter case run alone with a timer). `DW6`: a real Word file from Word and one from LibreOffice, each with headers, footers and an image, are accepted, and they render byte-identically to today. `DW7`: 1.5 M lone `{` is refused in under 2 s in every way in. `DW8`: the breaker re-runs every way in over HTTP. | **Source:** FO-5f item 5; AG-05 (templates); #1791 review rounds 1-4, breaker round 3 (the FO-5f2 hand-over in #1791's FO-5f screen folder); OWASP File Upload Cheat Sheet; Node `worker_threads` · **waits on:** FO-5f #1791 merged (`docx.ts`) · **not** `retainers/service.ts`, so it runs beside PF-6b · decisions: the allowlist and isolation design (Lead B, row owner, P-502; reversible) · **breaker: yes (availability for every firm); builder, reviewer and breaker at high effort** · **top of the front-office pull order (Lead 53)** · **size:** 1 PR · copied by Lead 53 from rows rev 16.15 (9cb0d9bcc) |
| **FO-3f** | runtime (booking hardening, FO-3's Minors) | 1. The name rule allows U+200C and U+200D between letters (a Persian name such as "علی‌رضا" books; m10). 2. The confirmation email goes through PF-14's `JobPort`, so a failed send retries after commit (m3). 3. `staffCreate` no longer returns the visitor's link token (staff never need it; m7). 4. One form stamp books once: a used stamp is refused with "This form was already sent. Reload the page to book again." (B-8 of the breaker; if no existing column can hold a used stamp, item 4 needs schema: it then leaves this row as **FO-3f2** with a migration slot from the release holder, and FO-3f lands without it). 5. The firm's 200 cap holds under bookings in flight: the count runs under a per-firm transaction lock (`pg_advisory_xact_lock` on the firm id) (breaker's cap race). | `api/src/front-office/booking/*.ts`, `tests/check_fo3_booking.ts` | each item has its pass and fail test; 20 parallel bookings at cap − 1 give exactly one success; `check_fo3_booking.ts` and the type check pass. | FO-3 on `main` (done), PF-14 on `main` (done) · Source: AG-4-1, AG-4-4, AG-4-5, AG-4-7, AG-4-9, AG-4-11, AG-4-12, AG-4-14, AG-4-16, AG-4-19, AG-4-22, AG-4-26, AG-4-29, AG-4-32, AG-4-34, AG-4-36, AG-4-37, AG-4-39, AG-4-47, AG-4-62, AG-4-63, AG-4-67, AG-2-10, AG-2-26; PR #1760 review and break reports (the items harden these rules) |
| **PF-4f** | runtime test (sign-in door checks, PF-4t's Minors) | In `tests/check_forms_qa53.ts`: the door rule asserts the exact status each door returns, not `status < 400`, so a 404 or 5xx fails (m1); the doors list is asserted to be exactly `["POST /api/platform/session/local"]` (m2). | `tests/check_forms_qa53.ts` only (a case-side file: **the release holder's OK is asked with this revision**) | a door answering 404 or 500 fails the check (shown once by a fail test); an added door fails the list check. | PF-4t (#1775, done) · Source: P-291, P-599; PR #1775 round 2 m1, m2 |

* **FO-3 size** (~1,000 lines against ~800): accepted once on #1760; no rule change.

**How:** each row's Source lists (1) the team's fine requirement ids it already cites, every one checked by script to exist in the newest name-free copy `domain/requirements-2026-11-02/` (shorthand such as "AG-4-16, -17" expanded; one slip, `AG-2-13` in FO-12s, dropped as not found); (2) the P-decisions that set or changed it (process rules such as P-223 and P-423 left out); (3) the regulator's Code with its official page and read date where the row cites it. FO-13k is left out (moved to PF-21 by P-584 (8)).

### Revs 16.31-16.39 (16 Nov 01:1x-02:4x IST, Lead B (6); copied by Lead 62 word for word from `lead-b-front-office` 6514b32bf, `design/lead-b-rows-2026-11-14.md`)
New rows named here (PF-18np, FO-7ts, PF-6sb, PF-0a, PF-6vt) take their text from these sections until Lead B writes their table rows. **Held by Lead 62 (16 Nov 04:3x IST):** rev 16.38's `ClientMoneyPort.addGovernmentFeeLine` conflicts with X-S3-6 ("One-click government-fee line from a case fee", P-607: typed in R1); FO-7b builds without it until the conflict is settled (P-607 is the founder's decision, AGENTS rule 2).

#### Rev 16.31 (16 Nov 01:1x IST, Lead B (6)): case-side shaping batch 1 answers (#1867, comment 6900265123)
* **FO-7mv / FO-7t split agreed.**
  - FO-7mv is the schema, the database guards and the derived list `money_to_move`, all tested at the database.
  - FO-7t is the move routes ("I moved these", "Undo this batch", the read with totals) and the 25-day home item. FO-7t calls `money_move_line_write` and reads `money_to_move` only, with no move rule of its own. It waits on FO-7mv, FO-7b and PF-12.
  - FO-7mv's brief is approved as row owner at `design/briefs/FO-7mv.md` (#1867). Lead B writes no second copy.
* **CS-DL1's move to `api/src/platform/calendar/working-days.ts` is OK,** for the general calendar logic only:
  - What moves: the business-day test, the zone helpers, `FirmCalendar`, `businessDaysBetween`, `countBack`, and `readFirmCalendar`.
  - What stays: `ACK_BUSINESS_DAYS` and `acknowledgeBy` remain in `complaints/business-days.ts`, which imports from calendar. That file is not removed and does not become a re-export.
  - It starts after PF-21v #1848. The PF-21, PF-21w and PF-21v suites must pass unchanged.
* **New row PF-18np** (runtime, a shared contract; the release Lead reads it):
  - It is `ports/notifications.ts`, zod plus a fake, with PF-18n's exact `notify(tx, {staffId}, kind, {kind, id})`.
  - `NOTIFICATION_KIND` is a typed list, with each kind added by its consumer.
  - The fake keeps one per person, per item and kind.
  - No preference check, no table and no routes: those stay with PF-18n, which may add to the interface but not change `notify`.
  - Tests: a duplicate gives one record, and an unknown kind is refused. Under 150 lines.
  - Once it merges, CS-DL1b, CS-NT1b and CS-N1 may build on it.

#### Rev 16.32 (16 Nov 01:1x IST, Lead B (6)): PF-19b booking mail swap confirmed (#1860, comment 6900280637)
* **Booking mail now runs on `MessagePort`'s `MessagesMail` in PF-19b part 1**, per the current Lead's brief (P-622 (1), qa #1855). That supersedes the PF-19b brief's line "do not move FO-3's `runtime-ports.ts`".
* **PF-19b part 2 reuses `MessagesMail`** (no second adapter). Its only move is from the demo outbox to SES: at most once, with the SENDING state (rev 16.25).
* **J-37 and J-38's swap list** marks booking mail as already swapped by PF-19b part 1.
* **A suppressed address is a final refusal, never retried.** The fix goes in #1860 if it fits the touch list, otherwise it is part 2's first item. Test: one record, zero further attempts, the booking unchanged.
* **Part 2 also takes**, each with a test: the orphan invite file and the unmailed manage link on a rerun, and the 64 KB inbox cap.

#### Rev 16.33 (16 Nov 02:0x IST, Lead B (6)): client-money batch 8 answers (#1878, comment 6900887428), against AG-06 v2.1
* **FO-7t's trust answer: Owner only, three states.**
  - The states are `UNANSWERED | YES | NO` (AG-6-59 §7–8). It is a role check in the service, since no key exists (MS-D24(4)).
  - Until YES the firm behaves as trust off.
  - FO-7a's boolean `trust_enabled` is replaced in FO-7ts.
* **The 25-day To-move item** counts a line's age from its source date, the withdrawal (AG-6-73 r5). The s. 32(g) reading is Unconfirmed: no counsel.

### Release 1 rows from the specifying lanes (P-604, 15 Nov; approved by Lead B (4))

**General rules (rev 16.11, Lead B (4), P-606), for every row below and every row already shaped:**

**(a) Every Done when line names its test: rule for every Lead B row, the Batch 5 rows and the qa rows**
* Each Done when line becomes **one named case** in the row's test file: `"<ROW> DW<n>: <the line in short>"`, numbered in the order the lines are written. Example: `tests/check_fo3h_*.ts` holds `"FO-3h DW1: host mail in host's office zone"`.
* A pass and a fail case share the number (`DW3 pass`, `DW3 fail`).
* The brief lists the case names before building (P-606 (4)), and the reviewer ticks one case per line. A line with no case is a red review finding, never a Minor.
* Journeys are named the same way in the join row's walk file (`J-<cap> W<n>`).
* This holds for every row already shaped (no row is rewritten; the builder adds the names in step 0 and the reviewer checks them), and for every new row.

**(b) One "join and walk" row per multi-part capability**
* A **J-row** is the last row of a capability. It owns:
  1. the **wiring** no part owns (QA-452's lesson);
  2. **every swap from a port fake to the real adapter** that the capability's running path needs (QA-453's lesson);
  3. the **screen**, if the capability has one, either built in the J-row or named as its one waits-on screen row;
  4. the **walk**: one browser journey from the capability's first step to the next capability's first step (gate A), in `tests/journeys/j_<cap>.ts`.
* qa scores the capability when its J-row merges (P-606 (2)).
* **Size:** S to M. It adds no new feature, only the joins.

**(c) Front-office screens first in the ready order**
No front-office screen exists yet. These go to the top of ready, each built on its API contract in parallel with the API row (P-606 (9)):
1. **FO-4** (sales screens; concept r2 approved) beside **FO-2** (its API).
2. **FO-3sc** (done: concept r2 merged in #1819, 15 Nov; Lead 62 16 Nov), then FO-3w (after FO-3pa #1830) and FO-3st1-3 (after FO-3a parts 1a and 1b).
3. **FO-5s** (concept r2 approved) after FO-6.
4. **PF-6** (staff screens; concept approved) beside PF-4b.
5. **PF-5s** after PF-5.

**From:** the three specifying lanes' drafts: S1 (platform and audit, branch `shaping/s1-platform-audit` 64ea121fb), S2 (front office and case side, `shaping/s2-front-office-case` 93db01193), S3 (client money and the "Not named" rows, `shaping/s3-money-unassigned` 651fa4ce2). Approved in batches 1 to 4 of Lead B (4)'s design/lead-b-shaping-approvals-2026-11-15.md (on branch lead-b-front-office, 590b104bb). Copied for Lead 52, 15 Nov.
**How they were copied:** each row as its lane wrote it, with Lead B's amendments applied word for word and marked "Amendment" (or "Lead B") inside the row. Lead B's six cross-lane amendments of batch 2 are numbered 1 to 6 and batch 4's are A to C, as in that file.
**How to read a row:**
* "Q" numbers and "question N" inside a row point to its own lane's question list (the S1, S2 or S3 draft file).
* S3's duplicate checks are written once per capability block (accepted by Lead B), in S3's §1 to §5.
* Every new schema row's migration test follows rev 16.7.
**Waiting on a founder question (built as drafted; if the founder says no, Lead B amends the row):** PF-8b (S1 Q1, the domain and its DNS), FO-7s1 (S3 Q4, the typed government-fee line), FO-7v (S3 Q2, trust never below zero), AS-2c (S2 Q5, "Review before sharing" on by default).
**Not copied here:**
* **The release holder's rows (now copied below, approved by Lead 52 under P-607):** AUD-1a, AUD-1b, AUD-1m, AUD-1c, AUD-2, AUD-2c, AUD-3, AUD-3b, AUD-4, AUD-4s, AUD-5; AS-1m, AS-1g, AS-1s, AS-1c, AS-2e, AS-2d1, AS-2d2, CS-106c; CS-98r, CS-98a to CS-98e, CS-98s1, CS-98s2; CS-D1, CS-D5c, CS-D5.

Rows below that wait on one of them (for example PF-10b on PF-F3) keep that wait.

**S1, batch 1: running it (map rows 1, 14, 10)**

| # | Area | Item | Done when (beyond the standard) | Source · Duplicate check · waits on · decisions |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| PF-8a | deploy (running it: the cloud estate as code, synth only) | **The Canadian estate as code, proven without an AWS account (map row 1).** A CDK v2 app in `deploy/cdk/` with its **own** `package.json` and lockfile (tooling outside the app, gate entry 8; the app's `package.json` is not touched, so no clash with PF-L): one stack per environment (`staging`, `production`), **region pinned to `ca-central-1`** in code. Network: a VPC over two availability zones, private subnets for the database and tasks, no public database. Database: RDS PostgreSQL (the version `main` runs in `compose.test.yml`), Multi-AZ in production, storage encrypted with a KMS key, deletion protection on, automated backups kept **35 days** (the RDS maximum; SA-2-21's and SA-11-32's "backups 35 days") with point-in-time restore, `rds.force_ssl` on, parameter `log_error_verbosity = terse` (rev 16.2, break 11). Files: S3 buckets (documents, exports, the import area) with public access blocked, versioning on, KMS encryption, TLS-only bucket policy. **A region guard:** `deploy/cdk/test/region.test.ts` fails on any resource, replication target or log group outside `ca-central-1`, except the backup copy in `ca-west-1` that PF-8r adds (C1-3's "CI region-allowlist"). CI job `deploy-synth` runs `cdk synth` and the CDK assertion tests on every change under `deploy/`, with **no AWS credentials** (synth needs none). **Touch:** new `deploy/cdk/**` (app, stacks, tests, `package.json`, lockfile, `cdk.json`, README), one job in `.github/workflows/skeleton.yml` (PF-7's workflow; release holder's OK as a shared file) | `cdk synth` succeeds from a clean clone with no AWS keys; assertion tests, a pass and a fail each: every resource's region is `ca-central-1` (a test stack with a `us-east-1` bucket → the guard fails, shown once, then removed); the database is not publicly reachable, encrypted, deletion-protected, backups 35 days; every bucket blocks public access, is versioned, encrypted and refuses plain HTTP; the production stack is Multi-AZ, staging single-AZ (cost, P-562); the job runs only when `deploy/` changes **Amendment (Lead B, batch 1):** the CDK app's `package.json` pins exact versions and its lockfile is committed; `npm audit --omit=dev` shows no high finding in the CDK tree. | **Source:** C1-3, NFR5-04, SA-2-21, SA-11-32, SA2-D12; RFP "Canadian data residency" (line 258) and "Backup & recovery"; P-559, P-562, P-586 · **Duplicate check:** BACKLOG names PF-8 with two Done-when lines only (rev 16.5: the task definition's environment and the smoke test; carried into PF-8b and PF-8c); no `deploy/cdk` on `main`; no open PR; no qa bug · **waits on:** nothing (synth only) · decisions: P-559 (AWS ca-central-1), P-586 (map row 14's targets), gate entry 8 (CDK) · reviewer only · **size:** 1 PR |

| PF-28 | runtime + CI (input checking on every PF/FO door) | **Every platform and front-office route checks its input, proven by a sweep (map row 10, the PF/FO half; the case side's half is CS-V1).** (1) **One rule** in `api/src/platform/http/input.ts`: a request body, query or path value reaches a service only through a zod schema (`z.strictObject`, so an unknown field is refused), with string lengths and array sizes bounded; a refusal answers the standard problem shape with 422 and fields, in plain words (400 only for an unparseable body, PF-0bp), an id that is not a uuid answers 404, never 500. Existing PF/FO services that already parse inside keep doing so; the rule only makes it checkable. (2) **A body-size limit** on the JSON parser for `/api/platform`, `/api/front-office`, `/api/system`, `/api/public` and `/api/hooks` (Sample 256 KB; file uploads keep their own per-route limits). (3) **The sweep** `tests/check_pf28_inputs.ts`: reads Nest's route table, and for **every** route under those prefixes sends, with a token holding the route's keys, an unknown field, a wrong type, a 1 MB string and a non-uuid id; each must answer 400, 404, 413 or 422 (an unknown field and a wrong type expect 422; a 1 MB string 413 or 422), never 2xx and never 500, and the database must be unchanged (row counts of the PF/FO tables before and after). A route that fails goes on an allow-list that **only shrinks**; the PR fixes every route that fails today, so the list starts empty if it can (each left entry named with its fix row). (4) Every new PF/FO route is swept automatically, so later rows inherit the check. **Touch:** new `api/src/platform/http/input.ts`, `tests/check_pf28_inputs.ts`; the one-line body-limit hook in `api/src/main.ts` (release holder's OK as a shared file); one-line schema fixes in the PF/FO route or service files the sweep finds (each listed in the PR) | tested with a pass and a fail each: a throwaway route with an unparsed body fails the sweep and passes once given a schema (shown once, then removed); an unknown field on `POST /api/platform/invitations` → 422 and nothing stored; a 1 MB string → 413 or 400; a non-uuid staff id → 404; a public door (`/api/public/...`) gets the same checks with no token; the allow-list is empty or every entry names its fix row; every existing PF/FO suite passes unchanged **Amendments (Lead B, batch 1):** (1) **webhook doors keep the raw body:** `/api/hooks/*` (Stripe now, a signing vendor later) verify a signature over the exact bytes, so their size limit sits on the raw-body parser and their zod schema runs **after** the signature check; a signed Stripe test event still verifies after PF-28; one with a single byte changed is refused 400. (2) `z.strictObject` on existing routes must not break the screens: every PF/FO browser suite runs on `main` merged in, and the results are pasted in the PR (rev 16's pre-label rule). | **Source:** AG-16-28, AG-18-57, AG-10-32; SA-11-2 (schema shape); Doc-1 config-as-data "validated by Zod at the edge" (ADR C1-2); STANDARDS §6; P-559 · **Duplicate check:** CS-V1 (`design/case-side-rows-draft2-2026-11-14.md`, not yet in BACKLOG) covers only `api/runtime` and `api/link`; zod 4 is already in `package.json`; PF/FO services parse inside (`api/src/platform/settings.ts`, `jobs/pgboss-jobs.ts`, booking `service.ts`) but routes pass raw bodies (`identity/routes.ts:16-45` pass `b ?? {}`), and nothing checks that every route parses; no BACKLOG row, open PR or qa bug on it · **waits on:** nothing (PF-4 is on `main`) · decisions: none · reviewer only (breaker if a public door changes) · **size:** 1 PR, more only if the sweep finds many gaps (then one PR per module) **Amended 18 Nov (Lead B rev 17.21, 46ae783cd; brief design/lead-b-briefs/PF-28.md):** field errors answer 422 with fields per RFC 9110; 400 only for an unparseable body or a bad webhook signature; 413 too large; 404 non-uuid or not visible; 409 state conflict; the sweep PR moves existing 400 field errors (PF-5t part 1's old version → 400 among them) to 422. |

| FO-3s1 | schema + port (front office: calendar) | **Calendar connection tables and `CalendarPort` with its fake.** The tables go in their own area file `front-office-calendar.prisma` with one migration:<br>• `fo_calendar_connection`: firm, staff, provider `GOOGLE`/`MICROSOFT`, token ciphertext from `SecretBoxPort` (PF-24; never plain), granted scopes, `busy_calendar_ids` text[], `booking_calendar_id`, status `CONNECTED`/`DEGRADED`/`FAILED`, `last_checked_at`, `broken_notified_at`, `connected_at`; unique `(firm_id, staff_id)` (one connection per person).<br>• `fo_calendar_event`: firm, booking, host staff, connection, `external_event_id`, `sequence`, state `PENDING`/`WRITTEN`/`FAILED`, attempts; unique `(booking_id, host_staff_id)`.<br>Both tables have composite `(firm_id, …)` foreign keys and join PF-12's walls.<br>**`ports/calendar.port.ts`** (zod; imports only `zod`) has: `authUrl(provider, state)`, `exchange(code)`, `listCalendars(conn)`, `busy(conn, from, to)` returning `{start, end}[]` (no titles in the type), `upsertEvent(conn, calendarId, event)` returning `{external_event_id, join_url?}`, `deleteEvent(conn, calendarId, id)`, `health(conn)`. The fake in `ports/fakes/calendar.fake.ts` has busy blocks set by the test, a forced-revoke switch and a call log.<br>The back-relations use reserved slot lines only: `PlatformStaff`/`PlatformFirm` in `platform.prisma`, and one line on FO-1b's booking model (P-600 (b)). **Touch:** `prisma/schema/front-office-calendar.prisma` + one migration folder, the slot lines, `ports/calendar.port.ts`, `ports/fakes/calendar.fake.ts`, `tests/check_fo3s1_calendar_schema.ts` | Tested with a pass and a fail each:<br>• a second connection for the same person is refused by the database;<br>• a token column holding text that is not `SecretBoxPort` ciphertext is refused by the check;<br>• `busy()`'s result type has no title field (a compile-time test);<br>• firm A's connection is invisible under firm B's `app.firm_id` once PF-12's walls are on (or a test under PF-12's pattern);<br>• a `fo_calendar_event` pointing at another firm's booking is refused by the composite key;<br>• the migration re-stamps cleanly after any earlier schema PR (P-600 (d)) **Amendment (Lead B, batch 1):** the back-relation on FO-1b's booking model goes **directly under that model's own slot comment** (rev 16.6); if FO-1b's model has no slot comment, the PR adds one there, and the release holder reads that line (P-600 (b)). The migration test follows rev 16.7: it measures right after its own migration and never assumes it is the newest. | **Source:** AG-4-40, AG-4-41, AG-4-42, AG-4-43, AG-4-56, AG-16-16, AG16-18, AG04-D12 · **Duplicate check:** FO-3s named only; no table, port, PR or code (15 Nov) · **waits on:** PF-0p (ports folder, done), PF-24 (`SecretBoxPort`; on `main`: `api/src/platform/secret-box/`), FO-1b (booking table) · decisions: P-570 (calendar sync is Build), P-585 (4) (Lead B's row), P-600 (parallel schema) · **breaker:** yes (schema, secrets) · **size:** 1 PR, ~350 lines |
| FO-3s2 | runtime (outside service: Google adapter) | **The Google adapter behind `CalendarPort`.** OAuth 2.0 authorisation code with PKCE and a signed `state` naming the firm and staff member, then token exchange and refresh. The least scopes: free/busy read, calendar list read, and event write (as the gate entry lists them). **Busy:** Google's free/busy query over the ticked calendars. **Events:** insert, patch and delete on `booking_calendar_id`. Meet links via conference data when the person's video choice is Google Meet (AG-4-29), returning `join_url`. A refresh failure that Google answers as revoked maps to `FAILED`; a timeout maps to `DEGRADED` with no state change to bookings. Tokens are never logged (PF-23's redaction list gains the token fields). **The library comes through the STANDARDS §1 gate first** (an entry in the PF-2 gate file; recommendation: Google's official Node auth library, or plain HTTPS through Node's `fetch` plus a certified OAuth client; P-587 gold-class), added by PF-L's one-PR rule. **Touch:** new `api/src/front-office/calendar/google.adapter.ts`, `…/oauth.ts`, `tests/check_fo3s2_google_adapter.ts` (recorded HTTP replies, no live call in CI) | Tested against recorded replies with a pass and a fail each:<br>• a forged `state` → refused, nothing stored;<br>• a reused authorisation code → refused;<br>• busy over two ticked calendars returns both blocks and drops all titles;<br>• an all-day event marked free does not block;<br>• insert then patch then delete hit the right calendar with SEQUENCE kept;<br>• "token revoked" → `FAILED`, a 5-second timeout → `DEGRADED`;<br>• a Meet link is returned only when chosen;<br>• no token appears in any log line (PF-23's scanner);<br>• one manual sandbox proof with a real Google test account is recorded in the PR (US$0) **Amendment A (Lead B, batch 4): one OAuth helper for Google and Microsoft (P-526).** Whichever of PF-19mc (mailbox) and FO-3s2/FO-3s3 (calendar) lands first builds it once in `api/src/platform/oauth/` (PKCE, signed state, exchange, refresh, revoke, tokens through `SecretBoxPort`), and the other reuses it; each row then adds only its own scopes and API calls. In the row that lands second: "no second PKCE or state implementation (a grep test)". One app registration per provider covers both features, so the founder files one Google verification (calendar plus the restricted Gmail scopes, with the yearly security assessment that Gmail needs) and one Microsoft publisher verification, not two each. | **Source:** AG-4-40, AG-4-41, AG-4-42, AG-4-43, AG-4-29, AG-16-16 · **Duplicate check:** none (15 Nov) · **waits on:** FO-3s1, PF-24, PF-23 (redaction), the gate entry + PF-L, the Google OAuth client (founder's browser, P-580) · decisions: P-587 (gold-class code), P-576 (5) (risky outside services early) · **breaker:** yes (outside service, tokens) · **size:** 1 PR, ~450 lines |

| FO-3s4 | runtime (booking: slots and writes use the connection) | **Busy times block slots; bookings are written to the calendar; broken connections fall back.**<br>(1) FO-3's slot service subtracts the host's busy blocks (60-second cache per host). Events Expat Ready wrote itself are ignored.<br>(2) After commit, a `JobPort` job (key `calendar:<booking>:<host>:<sequence>`) upserts or deletes `fo_calendar_event` on scheduled, rescheduled, host changed or cancelled. It retries with back-off and never touches the booking row. The event text is AG04-D12's only.<br>(3) A `FAILED` health check sends **one** email (`MailPort`) and makes one home item through `NotificationPort` (PF-18n), then stops reading and writing. a `firm.closure_held` line); (2) **requires the firm's full export** (PF-11) to be written to the write-once archive (`AuditArchivePort`, `firms/<firmId>/export-<date>.zip`, retention per AUD-5's longest class in it) and the download offered for all 90 days with the day-0, 30, 60, 85 notices sent (PF-5's notices; | PF-10b | runtime (platform: the import engine) | **One import engine with a mandatory dry run and an undo (map row 35; AG-18-1).** `ImportTargetPort.register(target)` in `ports/` (zod): a target declares its fields, a versioned row schema, its natural key, how it creates and merges (merge fills **only empty fields**, AG-18-38), its undo and its "worked on since" test (AG-18-41); the engine never branches on a target. Flow: upload (CSV UTF-8 or `.xlsx`, read as **values only**, formulas never evaluated, macro-enabled files refused, AG-18-6; Sample ceilings 10 MB, 5,000 rows, the refusal names the limit, AG-18-58; the file through `FilesPort`, and through `ScanPort` (PF-F3, platform); until PF-F3 merges, imports run on the scan fake in tests and are refused in production, else refused if a scan port is configured and not clean, AG-18-7) → map columns (one field per column, one column per field; allowed values mapped, an unmapped value makes the row an error, never a default, AG-18-8, -9) → **dry run** (every row against the target's schema; duplicates within the file and against the firm by natural key through FO-9a's `ports/matching.ts`; totals and per-row outcomes with reasons; AG-18-13, -15) → **run** only for a dry run of the same file hash and mapping (AG-18-16), as a `JobPort` job, **each row in its own transaction**, a failing row recorded without stopping, a crashed job resuming without duplicating rows (row key per batch and row number; AG-18-19, -20), every row re-validated (AG-18-17) → result report CSV (formula-safe, AG-18-21) and a PF-18n notification to the importer (AG-18-22) → **undo** within 14 days (Sample, AG-18-39) as an audited job removing what the batch created and restoring before-images, never touching a record worked on since (AG-18-40, -41). Imported records carry `origin = IMPORT` and the batch id (AG-18-42) and start **quiet**: an import fires no client-facing action (no message, invitation or automatic recipe; AG-18-43, -59, -60), internal work runs (AG-18-44, -61); consent is written only for a column mapped to one purpose with the importer's ticked declaration (AG-18-45, through `ConsentPort`); the Owner's attestation is required before any batch with personal data (AG-18-49); a file never supplies internal ids (AG-18-53). | PF-F1 | runtime (platform files: the cloud adapter, on fakes) | **Private storage in Canada behind the same `FilesPort` (map row 65).**<br>**Step 0:** a STANDARDS §1 entry for `@aws-sdk/client-s3` and `@aws-sdk/s3-request-presigner` (licence at source, version at least 14 days old) and for how CI tests the adapter with no network (a recorded fake client); Lead B approves it in the PR.<br>`api/src/platform/files/s3-files.ts` implements `put`, `get` and `exists`:<br>• **keys:** every object key is `<firm id>/<key>` (AG-9-17), and another firm's key reads as absent;<br>• **write-once:** the same bytes again are accepted; other bytes give `FileKeyTaken`, checked by a conditional put;<br>• **encryption:** every put sends server-side encryption with the KMS key named in the configuration (AG-9-18);<br>• **links:** short-lived signed GET links of 5 minutes (Sample), for one firm and one person (AG-9-16);<br>• **versions:** a new version is a new key (`…/v2…`); the bucket's own versioning only guards against a lost or overwritten object (PF-F1c).<br>The adapter is chosen by `PLATFORM_FILES=local\|s3`, which fails closed (rows rev 16.5): unset in production stops the start-up. The region is configuration, default `ca-central-1` (AG-9-19).<br>**Touch:** new `api/src/platform/files/s3-files.ts`, `api/src/platform/files/choose.ts`, product/design/tech-gate-files-s3-2026-11-15.md, `tests/check_pff1_s3_files.ts`; `package.json` (the two packages only) | On the recorded fake client, tested with a pass and a fail each:<br>• firm B's read of firm A's key gives null;<br>• the same bytes twice is accepted, and other bytes give `FileKeyTaken`;<br>• every put request carries the encryption header, and a put without it fails a check over the adapter;<br>• a signed link for another firm or another person gives 404, and one past 5 minutes gives 404 (clock injected);<br>• `PLATFORM_FILES` unset with `NODE_ENV=production` refuses to start;<br>• FO-5's existing tests pass unchanged with the local adapter. | **Source:** AG-9-16, AG-9-17, AG-9-18, AG-9-19; SA-8-6; P-559 (data in Canada) · **waits on:** PF-L (library entries, if it gathers them), STANDARDS §1 · **decisions:** P-559, P-586 (map row 14 storage in Canada) · **breaker:** yes (storage, security) · **size:** 1 PR |

| PF-F2 | schema + runtime (platform files: the upload rail, tus) | **Uploads that resume after a dropped connection, on tus (map row 64; batch 5 replaces the base's own session tables).**<br>• The platform's one upload rail on the **tus protocol** (an open standard for resumable uploads, P-502): server `@tus/server` with `@tus/file-store` locally and **`@tus/s3-store`** on PF-F1's bucket; client `tus-js-client` in `web/src/lib/resumable-upload.ts`; all three libraries through the STANDARDS §1 gate and P-587's checks in the PR (licence, upkeep, maintainers, Scorecard, recorded and not assumed), added by PF-L's one-PR rule.<br>• **Mounting and doors:** mounted at `/api/platform/files/uploads` (batch 2, amendment 4). **Doors register** with `UploadDoorPort.register(door, { authorize(ctx, meta), allowedKinds, onComplete(tx, file), onClean(tx, file) })`; the doors are `STAFF`, `CLIENT_LINK`, `CLIENT_PORTAL` and `SUPPORT`.<br>• **Every request checks access:** the upload id is made by the server and carries the firm, the door and the door's target (for a case door, the case and the checklist line or document type, taken from tus metadata); every tus request checks the session's firm and the door's `authorize`, and another firm's or another person's id answers 404.<br>• **At creation:** PF-F4's checks run on the declared `Upload-Length` and type, and nothing is staged on a refusal.<br>• **On finish:** PF-F4 checks the actual bytes; `FilesPort.put` stores the file under the door's key; `plat_file` is written as `SCANNING`, and PF-F3's scan job is queued; the door's `onComplete` runs; the staging copy is removed.<br>• **Expiry:** an unfinished upload expires after 24 hours (Sample; tus's expiration extension). A PF-14 job cleans it up, and **asks `LegalHoldPort` first**: if the person is held, the upload is kept and listed.<br>• **Storage:** `plat_file` is the only new table (`platform-files.prisma`: firm, key, sha256, size, media type, scan state, scanned at, signature name). tus keeps its own upload records, and the base's `plat_upload_session` and `plat_upload_part` are **dropped**.<br>• **Screen words** (from CS-D2): "Upload paused: reconnecting", "Resumed, 62%".<br>**Touch:** new `prisma/schema/platform-files.prisma` and its migration folder, `api/src/platform/files/uploads/*.ts`, `api/src/features/platform-files.ts`, `web/src/lib/resumable-upload.ts`, `tests/check_pff2_uploads.ts`, `tests/check_pff2_resume_browser.ts` | Tested with a pass and a fail each:<br>• a transfer cut mid-file resumes from the server's offset, and the finished file's sha256 matches;<br>• a repeated chunk is stored once;<br>• finished bytes that contradict the allowed kinds are refused, and nothing is stored;<br>• another firm's or person's upload id → 404;<br>• an expired upload cannot be finished;<br>• a held person's expired upload is kept;<br>• the declared size over the limit is refused at creation;<br>• a browser test cuts the network mid-upload and the file completes;<br>• the migration test follows rev 16.7. | **Source:** AG-9-1, AG-9-3 (per-file progress), AG-9-4 (same path on mobile); SA-8-58 · **waits on:** PF-F4, PF-F1 (for S3) or the local store, PF-14, PF-12, PF-21b (`LegalHoldPort`; its fake until then) · **decisions:** P-559; P-502 (tus, an open standard) · **breaker:** yes (schema, public door) · **size:** 2 PRs (the rail; the client library and the words) |
| PF-F3 | runtime (platform files: virus scan) | **Nothing is served while scanning (map row 66).**<br>(1) `ScanPort.scan(firmId, key) → CLEAN \| INFECTED(name) \| ERROR` in `ports/scan.ts`, with a fake: clean, except the EICAR test string, which is infected.<br>(2) A `JobPort` job scans every `plat_file` in `SCANNING` and moves it to `CLEAN` or `QUARANTINED`. On an error it moves to `SCAN_PENDING` and retries with back-off; it is never `CLEAN` by default (AG-9-21).<br>(3) **The gate:** `FilesPort.get` and every signed link refuse a file that is not `CLEAN`, **Batch 5: the gate moves inside `FilesPort` itself** (CS-D3's design, so no door can skip it): `FilesPort` gains `scanState(key)` and `markScanned(key, state)`, plus `getForScan(key)` for the scan job only; `get` and every signed link refuse anything not `CLEAN`, answering **409 "Still being checked"** while scanning and **410 "Removed: unsafe file"** when quarantined.<br>(4) A quarantined file is never served. The uploader's door is told through `file.quarantined` (an event in `ports/events.ts`) so the case side can re-request (AG-9-21). The firm's Owner gets one notice (SA-8-36).<br>(5) Scanner down for more than 10 minutes (Sample): one `AlertPort` alert to the engineer and one Owner notice (SA-8-36).<br>(6) `GET /api/platform/files/state?key=` gives the state words: staff see "Checking for viruses…", the client sees "Uploaded ✓" (AG-9-55).<br>**Step 0 (STANDARDS §1):** the real adapter, either a ClamAV daemon in its own container (GPL-2.0, run as a separate process over its socket protocol; licence reading Unconfirmed: no counsel) or AWS's managed malware scanning for S3 (availability in `ca-central-1` and data staying in Canada confirmed at AWS's own docs). Pick and reasons go in the PR (Q6).<br>**Touch:** new `ports/scan.ts`, `ports/fakes/scan.fake.ts`, `api/src/platform/files/scan/*.ts`, `ports/files.ts` and its adapters (the gate, batch 5), one line in `ports/events.ts`, `tests/check_pff3_scan.ts` **Batch 5 (consolidated; Lead 52's ruling, Lead B (4)):** added: `ERROR` retries three times, then the file stays `SCAN_PENDING` for staff (never `CLEAN`), with the base's alert; `INFECTED` is quarantined: kept for the audit and never served, staff are told in words and the uploader is told "This file could not be accepted"; the door's **`onClean`** runs in the scan job's transaction (where the case side writes `document.accepted`), only for a `CLEAN` file; the EICAR string is built at test time and never committed. **Scanner (Q6, ruled):** AWS's managed malware scanning for S3 if its own docs confirm `ca-central-1`, else ClamAV in its own container (clamd `INSTREAM`, behind a setting, off in CI, with one opt-in smoke test). | Tested with a pass and a fail each:<br>• EICAR becomes `QUARANTINED`, and `get` and the signed link both refuse it;<br>• a clean file becomes `CLEAN` and is served;<br>• a scanner error gives `SCAN_PENDING`, a retry, one alert after 10 minutes on the fake clock, and never `CLEAN`;<br>• the job run twice gives one state change and one event;<br>• the state words differ for staff and client;<br>• firm B asking firm A's state gives 404;<br>• no door can read a `SCANNING` file (a test walks every route that returns file bytes and finds none that skips `FilesPort`'s gate (kept as a second guard, batch 5)). **Batch 5 (consolidated; Lead 52's ruling, Lead B (4)):** "while scanning → 409; infected → 410; `onClean` runs once for a clean file and never for an infected one". | **Source:** AG-9-20, AG-9-21, AG-9-55; SA-8-36; SA8-D2 · **waits on:** PF-F2, PF-14, PF-23 (`AlertPort` adapter), PF-18n (Owner notice) · **decisions:** Q6 · **breaker:** yes (security) · **size:** 1-2 PRs (port, job and gate; the real adapter) **P-624 (16 Nov):** use AWS GuardDuty Malware Protection for S3 behind `ScanPort`, after one console check that it runs in ca-central-1. |
| PF-F4 | runtime (platform files: type and size check, pure code) | **One file-type and size check for every upload door (map row 67):** `api/src/platform/files/file-type.ts`.<br>(1) **Types are read from the file's own bytes, never from the name or the browser's claim.** Allowed: PDF (`%PDF-`), JPEG, PNG, HEIC (ISO-BMFF `ftyp` with a HEIC or HEIF brand), TIFF (`II*\0` or `MM\0*`), Word and Excel.<br>(2) Word and Excel are OOXML: a ZIP whose central directory holds `[Content_Types].xml` and a `word/` or `xl/` part. The central directory is read by our own code, with no decompression. Macro-enabled types (`.docm`, `.xlsm`) are refused.<br>(3) A plain **ZIP is refused in R1** with "Upload the files inside one by one" (Q7).<br>(4) Size limits are constants: 25 MB for documents, 10 MB for images (SA-8-58). They are checked against the declared size when a session opens, and against the actual bytes at the end.<br>(5) **Batch 5: the bytes decide the type.** A file whose name or declared type disagrees with its bytes is **stored under the bytes' type with its name corrected** (CS-D4's rule), and a double extension is cleaned. It is refused only when the bytes' type is not allowed at that door.<br>(6) The refusal message lists the allowed types (AG-9-2).<br>(7) `detect(bytes) → { mediaType, kind } \| refused(reason)` is exported for every door. The case side's `mediaOf` becomes a call to it in CS-D1.<br>**Touch:** new `api/src/platform/files/file-type.ts`, `tests/check_pff4_file_type.ts` plus fixture files under `tests/fixtures/file-types/` **Batch 5 (consolidated; Lead 52's ruling, Lead B (4)):** added from CS-D4: an image whose header declares more than **89.5 megapixels** (Sample; read from the PNG `IHDR` and JPEG `SOF` headers, no library) is refused, and nothing is stored; a PDF with active content (`/JavaScript`, `/OpenAction`, `/AA`, `/Launch`, XFA, embedded files) is **flagged, not refused**, because IRCC's own forms carry scripts, shown as "Contains scripts: opened only in the safe viewer" and detected by a byte scan with no library. The "damaged PDF" check needs a parser, so it stays in the case side's adoption (CS-D1, through the form engine's existing Python door); platform code never imports `engine/`. **Allowed kinds:** the platform knows the union (PDF, JPEG, PNG, HEIC, TIFF, Word, Excel); **each door narrows it** through `allowedKinds`; the case side's per-document-type list (`DocumentType.allowed_media`, default PDF, JPEG and PNG) is the case side's, in its adoption rows. | Tested with a pass and a fail each:<br>• one real sample of each allowed type passes;<br>• an `.exe` renamed `.pdf` is refused;<br>• HTML and SVG are refused;<br>• `.docm` is refused;<br>• a plain ZIP is refused with the "one by one" message;<br>• 25 MB + 1 byte as a document is refused, and 10 MB + 1 byte as an image is refused;<br>• a ZIP with a 2 GB declared entry is never decompressed (run time stays under 50 ms);<br>• the refusal lists the allowed types. **Batch 5 (consolidated; Lead 52's ruling, Lead B (4)):** a bomb PNG header is refused and stores nothing; a renamed JPEG is stored as `image/jpeg` with a `.jpg` name; an IRCC form PDF with scripts is stored and flagged; a door that allows images only refuses a PDF, with the allowed list in words; every refusal ends "Nothing was stored." | **Source:** AG-9-2, AG-9-2a, AG-9-54 (ZIP part held: Q7); SA-8-37, SA-8-49, SA-8-58; SA8-D2, SA8-D27 · **waits on:** nothing · **decisions:** P-607 (plain ZIP refused in R1, departing from SA-8-37 and SA-8-49) · reviewer only · **start now** · **size:** 1 PR (~400 lines with fixtures) |

| CS-D7c | schema + runtime (configurator: document-type defaults) | **Document types carry their translation, notarisation and validity defaults (AG-9-12).** `DocumentType` gains, all nullable (null means not set):<br>• `translation_rule` (`NOT_REQUIRED \| REQUIRED_IF_NOT_EN_FR`);<br>• `notarisation_rule` (`NOT_REQUIRED \| REQUIRED`);<br>• `has_expiry` (bool);<br>• `max_age_days` (int).<br>Each set value carries a `source_quote` and `source_file`, like `DocumentRequirement`, and goes through the existing governed library change with a second person's approval. This takes `L-doc`'s translation and validity parts.<br>**Touch:** `prisma/schema/library.prisma` (`DocumentType` only), one migration folder, `api/src/library-items.ts` (the four fields in its item schema), `tests/check_csd5c_type_defaults.ts` | Tested with a pass and a fail each:<br>• a draft type with `REQUIRED_IF_NOT_EN_FR` and a citation publishes after a second person's approval;<br>• one without a citation is refused at publish;<br>• a published type's values never change in place (a new version);<br>• old types read null and behave as today. **Amendment (Lead 52's review memo):** The migration test follows rev 16.7: it measures right after its own migration and never assumes it is the newest. The release holder reads the migration as the owner of that file (P-600). | **Source:** AG-9-12, AG-9-29, AG-9-64; P-170 (cited values) · **waits on:** the Bake tag (P-499), the configurator owner's OK · **decisions:** AGENTS rule 4 · **breaker:** yes (schema) · **size:** 1 PR |
| CS-D7 | schema + runtime + screen (case side: the flags on each document) | **Expiry, translation and notarisation flags on each document (map row 70).**<br>(1) The document record gains `language` (picked by staff at upload; the Reader's suggestion stays a suggestion, AG-9-64), `translation` (`NOT_REQUIRED \| REQUIRED \| PROVIDED` + detail), `notarisation` (the same), and `expires_on`. New nullable columns go in `evidence.prisma` with one migration.<br>(2) Defaults are seeded from the type (CS-D7c).<br>(3) When the language is neither English nor French and the type says `REQUIRED_IF_NOT_EN_FR`, the person's checklist gains one line, "Translation + translator's affidavit", linked to the document (AG-9-64). The wording is the type's cited source's; it is not shown until CS-D7c holds a citation.<br>(4) An expiry before today, or a document older than the type's `max_age_days`, shows a warning on the line, never a block (AG-9-64 age warning). Readiness (C7.6) reads the warning as an item, not a stop.<br>(5) A small panel on the document lets staff set and change the flags, audited.<br>**Touch:** `prisma/schema/evidence.prisma` (four columns), one migration folder, new `api/src/case-documents/flags.ts`, `api/src/case-checklist.ts` (the translation line hook, one place), `web/src/pages/case/document-flags/*`, `tests/check_csd5_flags.ts`, `tests/check_csd5_flags_browser.ts` | Tested with a pass and a fail each:<br>• a document in Spanish on a type with the rule adds one translation line, and setting it to English removes it (or marks it not needed with the reason "Language changed");<br>• the same document saved twice adds no second line;<br>• an expired document shows a warning, and nothing is blocked;<br>• a type with no rule adds no line;<br>• translation set to `PROVIDED` without a detail gives 400;<br>• every flag change writes one audit line;<br>• the panel works keyboard only, with axe-core at 0 serious. **Amendment (Lead 52's review memo):** The migration test follows rev 16.7: it measures right after its own migration and never assumes it is the newest. | **Source:** AG-9-12, AG-9-29, AG-9-64; AG07-D26(8) · **waits on:** CS-D7c, CS-D1 · **decisions:** AGENTS rule 4 (the line's wording only from a cited source) · **breaker:** yes (schema) · **size:** 2 PRs (schema with the API; the panel) |

**Module for map rows 68-70 (S3 §0; Lead 52, 15 Nov): case side (documents).** row 68 (Preview for staff review): It is the staff review of a client's document on the checklist (AG-9-24), and the documents module owns `evidence` (contract §2.4). row 69 (Read log, fingerprint, keep-period reminder): The read log is per document, read by someone who can see the case (AG-9-32, -53), and the fingerprint is per part (AG-9-31). Keep periods are Audit's (AUD-5, map 105), read through a port, so Audit owns the periods and documents owns the reminder. row 70 (Expiry, translation and notarisation flags): These are flags on documents and checklist lines (AG-9-12, -29, -64), seeded by the configurator's document types.

## Features (M2, P-191: journey order; regrouped 31 Oct)
A feature is done when a person can do it on the demo (:4400) and qa has tested it on `main`. One builder takes a feature end to end (screen, backend, tests); areas stay only as code ownership, so no two builders write the same files. The Next rows below stay as each feature's detail. Sizes are the tech lead's estimate for one builder: S about 1 day, M 2–3 days, L 4–6 days; they are corrected from real times after the first features.

| F | Feature (what a person can do) | Capabilities still open | Rows folded in | Builder | Size |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| F1 | **Set up the programme structure and each combination's card:** names on Program structure, Pathway type tabs, cards with their setup steps, channels and filing method, form-line copies, age and signers, who can be in the application, copy form and document lines between combinations (X2b; links between combinations left configuration, P-106: C1.3 is M3's case link, founder 31 Oct, PR #167) | C11.3, C11.16, C1.6, C1.8, C11.12, C1.5 | X1, X2 (X2b only), X4 (setup part) | screens | L |
| F2 | **Bring forms into the library:** the whole IRCC index, one guided path per PDF with progress and resume, same file and new edition handled, Start setup finds a combination's forms | C3.12, C11.11, C11.15, C2.12, C2.4 | X5 (slices a–c in flight), X8 (intake bugs), X10 (Adobe checks, evidence on the Mac) | forms | L |
| F3 | **Map form fields and trust each AI run:** AI maps fields, a reviewer accepts, edits or rejects with Undo, the validator, failures in plain words, live steps, progress, Stop and the cost warning | C3.1, C3.2, C3.3, C3.8, C11.8 | X6, X8 (mapping bugs), X8a | forms | M |
| F4 | **AI drafts the rest of a combination's masters:** checklists read into document lines, sections, document types, rules and checks drafted as proposals with a checker round, "Draft questions and documents", signed-scan lines | C2.6, C3.4, C3.6, C11.13, C11.14, C4.11, C3.11 | X7, X5 (checklist part) | forms (screens for its review pages) | L |
| F5 | **Keep the shared library tidy:** lists with where used, origin and status, rename a shared field, read-only checks and rules lists, remove, retire and restore | C3.13, C3.14, C3.15, C11.9 | X3, X4 (library part) | screens | M |
| F6 | **Build the questionnaires:** SQR per person role from what the forms and documents need, PQR picks (by hand and drafted by AI), Preview by person per channel, both inside the one review and release | C4.1, C4.2, C4.7, C4.6, C4.8 | X3 (QA-82 question words), X9 (questionnaire bugs) | runtime | M |
| F7 | **Demo reset and sample:** reset to blank, load the five combinations and the configured checkpoint | C10.1, C11.7 | N3 (#113), X9a, X9b | runtime | S |
| F8 | **Review and release without gaps:** an empty combination cannot be released; one To review page | X1a; C5.x already work | X1a, X4 (release part) | screens | S |
| R1 | **Thin runtime slice, rest:** every question type in the one renderer; a filled form passes Adobe Validate | C4.4, C7.2 | X9, X10 (E1b) | runtime (+ evidence on the Mac) | M |
| Gate | **M2 gate:** the content publisher walk on the six combinations, like a person | all of the above | X11 | qa | S |

**Order and parallel plan (three cloud builders, one qa):** forms F2 → F3 → F4; screens F1 → F8 → F5; runtime F7 → F6 → R1; qa after each day's merges, then the gate. The forms line is the longest (about 9–15 builder-days), so the M2 gate is about three weeks out on today's estimate; the one Mac check queue can stretch that, and the first features' real times firm it up.

**M3 features (after the M2 gate; design agreed), in the order a case moves:** M3-A firm, users and case team; Retail and Corporate apart (C6.11, C11.5) · M3-B filings and fees from the case; channel per form within what IRCC accepts (C6.12, C11.6) · M3-C documents: checklist per person, AI reads uploads into facts to confirm (C6.4, C6.5) · M3-D staff eligibility page (C6.9) · M3-E prepare and check: deliverables list, ready-to-file rules, independent AI review, portal sheet (C7.7, C7.6, C7.5, C7.3) · M3-F submit and keep it safe: signed scans, filing records, stale marks, the finished demo story (C8.1, C8.2, C9.2, C10.2).
